--- Model Response ---
1. The two black nodes are A and T.
2. The immediate neighboring nodes of A are S, E, and F.
3. The immediate neighboring nodes of T are F, B, and G.
4. Nodes E, F, and B are key connectors:
   - Node E connects S to E to A.
   - Node F connects to A and also has connections to B.
   - Node G connects to T and B.
5. There are multiple paths between A and T. Any path will intersect E, F, or B.
6. Deductions:
   - E is a pivotal, but removing E alone may not suffice. We need to evaluate what’s left after removing E.
   - F is a direct connection node. Removing F indirectly connects through B, and such, making removal essential.
   - B is another essential node. Removal of B essentially severes multiple potential paths between A and T.
7. Removing one of (E, T), (F, T), and (B, G) is not enough. So the key is removing F and E to disconnect T and A entirely.
8. After reconfirming via paths intersect through E and F as bottlenecks, we conclude:
Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, T), (F, T)].

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the Black Nodes:**
   - The black nodes are S and O.

2. **Identify Immediate Neighboring Nodes:**
   - The immediate neighboring nodes of S are B and E.
   - The immediate neighboring nodes of O are Q and G.

3. **Examine Connections of Neighboring Nodes:**
   - **Neighbors of S:** B and E.
     - B connects to S, F, T, and E.
     - E connects to S, B, F, and A.
   - **Neighbors of O:** Q and G.
     - Q connects to O, P, and J.
     - G connects to O, C, R, B, I, and J.

4. **Trace the Paths:**
   - From S to O via B and G:
     - S -> B -> F -> T -> G -> O
   - From S to O via E and Q:
     - S -> E -> B -> G -> O
     - S -> E -> F -> T -> G -> O
   - From S to O via B and Q:
     - S -> B -> T -> Q -> O

5. **Identify Bottlenecks:**
   - The common nodes in all paths are B and G. Removing these nodes will disconnect S and O.

### Conclusion:
Removing nodes B and G will completely disconnect the black nodes S and O.

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, G]

   - For Q, we should remove `(Q, J)` to disconnect Q.

To fully disconnect H and Q, we need to remove both `(H, G)` and either `(Q, J)` or `(Q, I)`.

   - Removing `(H, G)` and `(Q, I)`: This leaves no path between H and Q.
